# PLAN — the single source of truth (projects · tasks · ETAs)

**This file is THE plan.** Every task lives here, under a project, with an `ETA` date and a `status`.
The long-horizon plan (the 6/22 launch) lives here too — as dated tasks, not a separate doc. There is no
second plan surface: the calendar is phone reminders only, the harness #-task list is a session scratch
mirror, `CHANGELOG.md`/git hold history. If it's planned, it's a line here.

**Line format** (strict — parsed by `scripts/check_plan.py`):
`- #<id> | <YYYY-MM-DD ETA> | <status> | <title>`   ·  status ∈ pending · in_progress · blocked

**The two rituals operate ONLY on this file:**
- **OPEN** (every session): run `python scripts/check_plan.py --today` → it prints OVERDUE + due-today open
  tasks. That list IS the day's plan. State it before reacting to anything.
- **CLOSE** (every session): every open task's ETA must be ≥ today. Rebump any past-due one to a real
  future date (or close it). File every new item as a line here under a project. Then commit — the
  pre-commit gate (`check_plan.py`) REFUSES the commit if any open task lacks a project, an ETA, or has a
  past ETA, or if an open harness task is missing from this file. No gaps, mechanically.

_Last CLOSE: 2026-06-16._

---

Our system only ever looks on the gap day. ⚠ Measured so far, and the bar to beat: the supply read is a NULL as a winner predictor (0.496 at matched dollar volume, n=2,787) and adds NOTHING beyond the existing extension rule on his reject bar (8/11 rejected, 0/26 wrongly — the extension gate alone gets there). Its one unique save is CAR 04-01. Evidence: `docs/analysis/structure_read_{v2,v3,backtest}_2026-08-25.md`. ⚠ **Guard against fitting**: at 15 labels a favourable cutline is trivial to find and worthless — every future number states which parameters were pre-declared. 🔁 **CADENCE — operator 2026-08-25: *"chart read should be periodic and shadow, every now and then we review good and bad charts together, refine, also making sure our running list of real EPs aren't impacted negatively."*** So: (a) it runs in SHADOW continuously, recording its read per candidate — never gating anything; (b) it surfaces a REVIEW SAMPLE periodically — good calls AND bad calls together, not just failures — on a recurring `data_gated_reviews.yaml` entry so the cadence is mechanical and not remembered; (c) **every refinement is re-checked against `tests/fixtures/must_not_miss_eps.py` BEFORE anything else** — the running list of real EPs may never shrink, and `tests/test_577_must_not_miss_eps.py` already fails by name on a new exclusion, so that guard exists and must stay in the loop. ⚖ Shadow only; nothing touches admission without CHANGE_PROCESS + sign-off. His eye is STRONG at refusing losers and MIXED at catching winners — precisely the asymmetry RULE 0 assumes.** ▶ 🥇 **RNG 2026-07-24 IS THE FIRST DATE HE HAS EVER CALLED OUTRIGHT GOOD — and we REJECTED it (score 37 < 50, catalyst=routine).** His four reasons are all structural and NONE is in our score: *"a long base since 2022"*, *"this gap cleared most of it into top of previous range highs over multiple years"*, *"closed near high of day"*, *"moved above all moving averages on gap day"*. ▶ **THE CRITERIA HE ACTUALLY APPLIED, read off the ten:** **(1) did the gap CLEAR overhead supply — the first question in six of ten, and measured against MULTI-YEAR structure (a 2022 base, an Oct-2025 double top); (2) WHERE IT CLOSED IN THE DAY'S RANGE — NVTS's tell, a day-0 computable fact we do not score; (3) position vs the 10/20/50/200 MAs — enough on its own to lift OMER from garbage to "ok'ish" despite failing (1); (4) the prior TREND — MRLN is condemned on it before anything else.** ▶ ⚠ **TWO MORE DATE CORRECTIONS, the recurring finding: CGEM's real day is 06-22 not 06-08; AVAH's is 08-13 not 06-02 — and OMER has a SECOND approved gap on 08-13. THIRTEEN MONTHS.** Aggregating that into weeks gives ~57 candles, into months ~13. **That is the same 13 months drawn coarser — it cannot show a pattern that predates the window.** ▶ **MEASURED AGAINST HIS OWN RULINGS FROM THE SAME SESSION: RNG's *"long base since 2022… cleared most of it into top of previous range highs over multiple years"* — our earliest RNG bar is 2025-08-04, 275 bars. **THE ENTIRE BASIS OF THE ONLY CHART HE HAS EVER CALLED OUTRIGHT GOOD IS OUTSIDE OUR DATA.** NVTX's *"double top from Oct 2025"* — earliest bar 2025-09-09, so it sits barely inside the window with no prior context to judge it against.** ▶ ⚠ **AND THIS REFRAMES THE 2026-08-25 STRUCTURE-READ NULL (0.496 at matched dollar volume, n=2,787) THAT THIS TASK TREATS AS ITS BAR TO BEAT: it was computed on ≤13 months of bars while he judges multi-year structure. A NULL MEASURED ON A WINDOW THAT CANNOT CONTAIN THE PATTERN IS NOT EVIDENCE THE PATTERN IS ABSENT.** [[shadow-zero-effect-check-instrumentation]] ▶ **THE FIX IS A MULTI-YEAR DAILY BACKFILL, not a chart-interval change — Polygon and Alpaca both serve years of daily history and we already pay for both. After that, weekly/monthly aggregation becomes real rather than a relabelling.** ⚖ **HIS CALL on scope: how many years, and which universe (the ~30 labelled names, the alert population, or everything).** I can price it once he says which. Detail: `operator_shared_notes.md` §"switch to higher time frame". >> 💰 **2026-09-06 — 5 YEARS PRICED AND VERIFIED REACHABLE. IT IS CHEAP.** ▶ **API: ~1,260 calls TOTAL, not per ticker** — Polygon's grouped-daily endpoint returns the WHOLE universe for one session in one call, so universe size does not affect API cost at all. **Flat subscription → $0 marginal.** ▶ **DEPTH VERIFIED LIVE from the prod container rather than assumed: 2021-09-07 → HTTP 200 with **10,580** tickers · 2023-09-06 → 200, 10,466 · 2025-09-05 → 200, 11,401. Our plan reaches 5 years.** ▶ **STORAGE: `mi_daily_closes` is 3.26M rows / 555 MB over 275 sessions. 5 years ≈ 1,260 sessions = **4.6×** → **~15M rows, ~2.5 GB**. Prod has **49 GB free** (33% used), so it is 5% of headroom.** ▶ **Runtime: paced politely, ~20-30 min for a one-shot backfill.** ▶ **NO REASON TO NARROW THE UNIVERSE: grouped-daily returns everything per call, so filtering saves storage but not calls — and 2.5 GB is not worth optimising against future questions we cannot predict.** ⚖ **Recommend the full 5 years, full universe. Awaiting his go.** >> 🚀 **2026-09-06 — BACKFILL APPROVED AND RUNNING, AND HE HAS NAMED WHAT IT IS FOR.** ▶ **RUNNING: `scripts/probes/_backfill_daily_history.py --years 5` on prod, detached.** Dry-run first: 275 sessions held, **1,019 weekday sessions to fetch**, target start **2021-09-07**. **REUSES the live path rather than re-implementing it** — `collector.get_grouped_daily` + `db.ingest_daily_closes` — so backfilled rows are identical in shape to the nightly job's (same ticker filter, same conflict target, same COALESCE that never overwrites good OHLC with a null). **Only touches dates we do NOT hold; the upsert is idempotent so an interruption is harmless.** Progress at 100/1019: **1.01M rows, 3 holidays skipped, ZERO failures.** ▶ 💡 **HIS IDEA FOR WHAT IT UNLOCKS, flagged by him as POTENTIAL, recorded as a direction not a commitment:** *"we can expand the review and do more open ended chart vision reading, look at what bases look like, what break out looks like, what holds up and how it correlates with EPs we find."* ▶ **THE SHIFT IS FROM JUDGING TO CHARACTERISING: sample #1 asked him a closed question about ten names we picked. This asks what the shapes ARE.** Four distinct studies in his four clauses: **(1) a taxonomy of the BASES that precede EPs — we have no vocabulary for this beyond `extension_pct`; (2) what a real BREAKOUT looks like versus a gap that stalls — RNG is the worked example; (3) what HOLDS UP, and he measures it PER DAY (*'still ok until 3rd day'*, *'still holding up'*), not as one outcome; (4) HOW IT CORRELATES WITH OUR EPs — the join that turns chart study into a selection input.** ⚠ **THE TRAP, and it is 2026-09-05's lesson: ~30 labelled names plus a free hand over multi-year charts makes a favourable pattern trivially findable and worthless. Any shape found by looking must be DECLARED BEFORE it is measured, and measured on the TAIL ([[measure-the-tail-not-the-median]]).** ⚠ **And checked against `must_not_miss_eps.py` first — the real-EP list may never shrink.** Verbatim: `operator_shared_notes.md` §"open-ended chart reading". >> 📖 **2026-09-06 — HE SENT A MODEL-BOOK PAGE (AMZN 2020, TraderLion) AND IT READS PERFECTLY. He has DOZENS more and will scan them if useful.** ▶ **SIX THINGS THIS ONE PAGE NAMES THAT WE DO NOT COMPUTE:** **(1) THE 23-EMA IS THE SPINE —** four separate annotations: every add is stopped at it, the trend is *"clear respect for its 23-EMA throughout the length of its move higher"*, and the run ends when price closes below it on volume. **We compute no 23-EMA anywhere; our stop is `entry−2R` off a ONE-MINUTE range while this trails an MA for the life of the move.** **(2) A NAMED BASE VOCABULARY** — pocket pivots (*"5-day PP"*, *"PP volume signature"*), prior resistance becoming support, *"tight, sideways on light volume"* as the constructive state. **This IS the taxonomy he asked for two messages earlier, already written down.** **(3) RSNHBP — relative strength making new highs BEFORE price, and throughout the base.** We rank RS percentiles; we never check whether RS leads price. **(4) BEHAVIOUR IN A CORRECTION AS A QUALITY SCREEN** — −25.61% against a −35.41% market. Cheap to compute, absent from our stack. **(5) VOLUME ABSENCE AS A NEGATIVE** — *"volume was below average as AMZN made new highs, which is a sign of weakness"*. We treat volume as positive-only. **(6) THE EXIT IS A GRADED SEQUENCE, NOT A LEVEL** — extended above the 10-DMA + big-volume negative reversal → partials; closing below 23-EMA/50-DMA → sell some or all; volume expansion on the first close below the 23-EMA since breakout → run over. **Three signals, none an R multiple.** ▶ 📊 **AND THE FUNDAMENTALS STRIP CONTRADICTS OUR RUBRIC: Q1-2020 EPS FELL 29% and AMZN was still a model-book leader** (revenue 21→26→40→37%, EPS −29→+97→+192%). **A single weak EPS quarter did not disqualify it — our axis-2 EPS trajectory would have marked it down.** ⚠ **ONE page, one name, and a winner chosen FOR a model book — survivorship by construction. Source of HYPOTHESES to declare then test, never evidence.** >> 🔬 **2026-09-06 — MULTI-YEAR CLEARANCE TEST RUN ON THE NEW HISTORY. THE PRE-DECLARED MEASURE FAILS, AND IT FAILS INFORMATIVELY.** ▶ **Declared IN THE PROBE BEFORE any number was computed** (`scripts/probes/_594_multiyear_clearance.py`): `clearance_pct` = the % of daily HIGHS in the prior 5 years that the GAP-DAY CLOSE exceeds. One number, no knobs, no alternates tried — the literal reading of *"cleared most of it into top of previous range highs over multiple years"*. ▶ **RESULT: approved median **84.7%** (n=6) vs BAD_CHART median **97.3%** (n=17) — a separation of **−12.6 points**. INVERTED. His bad charts clear MORE overhead supply than the ones he approves.** ▶ 🔑 **WHY, and this is the useful part: six of the seventeen BAD names sit at 99-100% — AVGU, CAR 04-21, QH, AEHR, MRAM, NVTS all gapped to effectively NEW HIGHS, and he called every one of them bad. Meanwhile RNG, the only chart he has ever called GOOD, is at 80.2% — NOT at new highs, exactly as he described it (*"cleared MOST of it"*).** **So a clearance percentile is NOT what he means. A junk stock gapping to an all-time high scores 100% on it. What he is judging is the QUALITY AND SHAPE of the base being cleared, not the fraction cleared.** ▶ ⚠ **POST-HOC OBSERVATION, FLAGGED AS SUCH AND NOT A FINDING — I noticed it in the output, it was NOT declared, and reporting it as evidence would be the exact trap I wrote into this line yesterday: the approved names have **1,224** prior bars at median (minimum 1,146) while the BAD names have **655** (minimum 150), and **7 of 17 bad names have under 400 bars versus 0 of 6 approved**. i.e. many of his "garbage" calls are recent listings with no multi-year base to speak of. THAT IS A HYPOTHESIS FOR A DECLARED TEST, NOT A RESULT.** ▶ **NET: the backfill was still worth it — this test was IMPOSSIBLE two hours ago (RNG had 275 bars, now 1,224) and the 0.496 structure null still deserves a re-run on 5 years. But the first structural measure we could finally compute does not separate his labels.** >> ⚠ **2026-09-06 — THE BACKFILL INTRODUCED A MONEY-PATH LATENCY REGRESSION AND I CAUGHT IT BEFORE PARKING. FIXED.** ▶ **`mi_daily_closes` went 3.3M → 13.6M rows, and its only index was `idx_daily_closes_ticker` — TICKER ONLY. So every per-ticker window query fetched EVERY bar for that ticker and filtered by date afterwards: a single-ticker lookup went to **111 ms** on prod (EXPLAIN ANALYZE, not estimated).** ▶ 🔴 **THAT SHAPE IS ON THE MONEY PATH: `backtester/filters.compute_atr_14` runs it PER CANDIDATE during the 09:31-09:44 ORB window, so ~20 candidates meant roughly 2 SECONDS of avoidable latency sitting in front of order submission** — and ATR feeds `validate_orb_entry`'s stop-width gate. ▶ **FIX: composite `idx_daily_closes_ticker_date ON mi_daily_closes(ticker, trade_date)`, created CONCURRENTLY on prod (no lock) and added to the schema DDL in `db.py` so a rebuild or a disaster-recovery restore recreates it.** ▶ **MEASURED AFTER: the general query 111 ms → **2.09 ms** (53×); the ATR shape → **0.131 ms**.** ▶ **Also checked before parking, and all clean: tree committed, both probe scripts (`_backfill_daily_history.py`, `_594_multiyear_clearance.py`) are IN the repo rather than only in the container, and all 32 `FROM mi_daily_closes` call sites in `db.py` carry a `trade_date` bound — none was left unbounded to scan the new 13.6M rows.** ⚠ **The lesson worth keeping: a data backfill is a performance change. I priced storage and API calls up front and did NOT price the read path, which is where the only real risk was.** ▶ ⏸ **PARKED 2026-09-06 at a clean point, on the operator's check. THE TRIGGER TO RESUME IS HIS SCANS, not a date — he is scanning the rest of the model book (*"i'll work on scanning those… will take some time"*), and those pages are the only input that materially changes what can be learned. 2026-09-20 is a REVIEW date, not a deadline.** ▶ 🔴 **AND THE REAL ANSWER TO *"when should we pick this up"* IS A GAP: this line says the loop should run on *"a recurring `data_gated_reviews.yaml` entry so the cadence is mechanical and not remembered"* — **THAT ENTRY DOES NOT EXIST.** Both chart-related reviews in the YAML (`chart_vision_axis_shadow_decision`, `ep_cooldown_resetup_admission`) are `status=done`. **So today the cadence depends entirely on me remembering, which is precisely what the task says it must not.** Creating that entry is the fix and it is small.** ▶ **What can proceed WITHOUT his scans, both small: re-run the 0.496 structure null on the 5-year history (it was measured on 13 months and is this task's stated bar to beat), and properly DECLARE-then-test the base-length hypothesis I flagged post-hoc today (approved names 1,224 prior bars median vs 655 for bad).** [b4] [blocked:the next sample waits on chart_reading_review_cycle — 20 new EP alerts after 2026-10-05, about four weeks at the live rate] revalidated:2026-10-05 ▶ ✅ **CADENCE ENTRY BUILT 2026-09-06 — the gap is closed. `chart_reading_review_cycle` is now in `data_gated_reviews.yaml` (153 entries, ids unique), `kind: cadence`, threshold 40 new alerts since 2026-09-06, earliest 2026-09-20.** So the loop no longer depends on me remembering — which is what this line always said it must not, and what it silently did until he asked. ▶ **The entry carries the METHOD, not just a date: build the sample BALANCED BOTH WAYS (HIGH-that-collapsed AND below-bar-that-ran) because his *"our chart vision is spotting the opposite thing"* was only visible BECAUSE both directions were shown; [Operational safety / hardening] >> 🔴 **ROOT CAUSE FOUND 2026-09-11 — it is not a scheduling gap, it is `execute_full_exit` itself (`broker/order_manager.py:~4728`).** The function does, in this order: **(1) cancel the stop order · (2) `alpaca.close_position(...)` · (3) on any exception, Telegram *"Full exit FAILED"* and `return False`.** **There is no rollback and no stop replacement on the failure path — the position is left naked BY CONSTRUCTION every time step 2 fails.** ⛔ **AND STEP 1 IS WHAT MAKES STEP 2 FAIL.** The rejection was *insufficient qty available (requested 2, available 0, held_for_orders 2)* — Alpaca had not yet released the shares the just-cancelled stop was holding. **The cancel and the sell race each other, and the function causes its own failure.** ▶ **SEVERITY: this is not confined to after hours.** Any full exit — including a real stop-driven exit mid-session — can lose its stop and keep its shares while price is moving. Themes = no-money detection (ships full); re-granularization THRESHOLDS carry CHANGE_PROCESS N≥10._

The stock was +32.6% by 09:35 and +33.5% by 09:40. **ALPACA'S OWN WORDS, recovered:** `"reason": "[6098] Stop Price Already Triggered/Exceeds $ Threshold"`. **WHERE IT HID:** not on the order object (checked every key of the raw REST payload) — it is on the trade-updates EVENT beside the order, and the SDK's `TradeUpdate` model has NO `reason` field, so `raw_data=False` parses it away before our handler runs. Every rejection we have ever had arrived with a reason we threw out. ⚠ **Hours were spent inferring** — ruling out buying power ($17,355 vs a $910 order), account state, symbol restrictions, an in-the-money trigger, and a paper probe (`scripts/probes/_540_oto_rejection_probe.py`, 6 cases) that DID find a real rule (`stop_loss.stop_price must be <= base_price - 0.01`, base_price = the LIMIT price) which **does not explain INSM** (126.15 ≤ 130.05 passes). ⚠ **An external research pass proposed 'market price was below the stop-loss' — FALSIFIED by our own data**: last was $128.96, above the $126.15 stop. **SHIPPED:** `alpaca_client.fetch_broker_reject_reason()` — a targeted lookup against the trade-events history, called ONLY on reject/cancel/expire, never on the fill path; the reason lands in the audit detail as `broker_reason` AND in the operator Telegram. Deliberately NOT `raw_data=True`: that would rewrite every handler on the FILL path to work on dicts to recover a field needed only on a terminal failure. Never raises; a missing reason degrades silently. 7 tests. So the mechanism is wired and the lookup does not find what a manual query finds. ⚠ **Do not close this on the INSM test** — that proved the function can read a `rejected` event after the fact; this is a `canceled` event, read live, and it came back empty. Candidate causes, none confirmed: the 4-second window is too tight for a 6 ms cancel whose event may not be queryable yet; or the handler filters on `rejected` and a `canceled` event never reaches the lookup. **DoD: the next broker cancel OR rejection carries a non-null `broker_reason` in its audit row and Telegram — proven on a real live event, not a replay.** This is the exact defect class the task exists to close: the broker told us why, twice, and both times we threw it away. WOULD-FAIL-IF: demotion requires a human to notice, which is the failure mode a ladder exists to remove. ⚖ THE LINE: any live rung needs operator sign-off.** ⚠ This is a ROADMAP CONTAINER — its bar is that the named work lands or is ruled out, not that a single artifact appears. >> 🔴 **SECOND PRE-FLIP GATE, FOUND 2026-09-14 — THE PHASE-1A TELEMETRY CANNOT VALIDATE THE FLIP, BECAUSE THE ALLOCATOR RUNS AT 9:35 ET AND ORB ENTRIES SUBMIT AT 9:31.** `docs/analysis/unified_allocator_phase_1b_2026-09-14.md`. `cross_strategy_allocator.py:252` reads `get_open_position_count()` LIVE at 9:35, so `slots_available` already excludes that morning's fills — and **6 of the 7 winners that ever filled had ALREADY FILLED when the ranking was computed** (ABCL 09:34 · BW 09:31 · ETON 09:31 · AMLX 09:32 · PHVS 09:31 · DFTX 09:33; only OKTA 09:49 came after). **Each of those six is counted TWICE — once inside `open_positions` consuming a slot, once as the winner of the slots that remain, so a flip at THIS TIMING would over-allocate. ⚠ That is an artifact of running AFTER the fills, not a sizing defect in the allocator — the timing move removes it.** The review's own comparison (audit `winners` vs actual fills) is therefore circular: agreement is guaranteed when the winners are drawn from names already filled. ⚠ Four months of telemetry answer nothing, and more days will not help — winner trade-through is **5 of 19 (26%)**, the same ~1/6 interception rate that deferred this on 2026-05-18, and on 7 of 9 contested days FEWER names entered than there were slots (39 candidates, 19 slots, 8 entries) so the 5-position cap is not what decides these books. ▶ **THE PRECONDITION: move the shadow job 9:35 → 9:28 ET and fix the double-count, shadow-only, submits nothing, $0** — then the FCFS-vs-allocator question becomes askable for the first time. ⛔ It changes WHEN a money-path job reads the book, so it ships with the numbers and **his sign-off (THE LINE)** — like the RED-3 clamp above, it must land BEFORE any Phase-1B flip. ✅ **The move was checked before being recommended: a 9:28 job does NOT read an empty queue.** Since #515 the only writer is `enqueue_pending_allocation` (`ep_detector.py:5999`) on an EP scan that runs every 5 min from 07:00 ET, and **19 of 27 queue rows across five sampled days were already present by 09:28**. ⚠ **Its cost, stated: the other 8 arrive AFTER 09:28** — post-open upgrades from the dedicated 9:31 `ep_scan_open` job — so a 9:28 allocator ranks the field EARLY but INCOMPLETE. The richer alternative is the 2026-07-03 doc's §9.3 (read slots live at each candidate's own attempt time), which keeps the full field and costs more. ✅ **HE RULED 2026-09-14: "aligned, A, then B if this becomes necessary" — SIGNED. Do A (move the shadow job to 9:28 ET + fix the double-count), and only build B (slots read live at each candidate's own attempt time) if A's incomplete-field cost shows up in the data.** ⚠ A is money-path TIMING, so it goes in a WEEKEND build slot (Sat 2026-09-19 or Sun 09-20), not a market day; it stays shadow-only and submits nothing. ⚠ **What would make B necessary, written down now so it is not decided after the fact: the post-9:28 arrivals turn out to be the ones that WIN the slots** — concretely, if over 15 contested post-move days the names entering after 09:28 would have outranked the 9:28 winners on more than a third of those days, A is judging the wrong field and B is owed. If they rarely outrank, A is sufficient and B is closed.  ⚠ #415 (allocator telemetry) shipped the eligibility flag and cascade info but its third piece, the operator's *"slots snapshot cadence"*, shipped as ONE value at the cron tick and closed on *"all 3 fields present"* (`731ea850`) — partially shipped, closed on the narrower reading; named as context, not reopened. 🔴 **MEASURED 2026-09-15 on his question (*"how do we determine priority of slots? it's based on score right?"*) — YES, AND THE +10 THEME BONUS IS 40% OF IT.** `ep_score` -> `score_magna53` -> `composite = 0.40*setup + ...` -> the allocator's rank. **Over 141 queued rows / 31 days: the cap actually bound on 14 days, and removing the +10 changes the WINNER SET on 2 of them (14%)** — 07-31 BLZE over MPWR (moot: both skipped downstream), 08-14 ETON over VERA (**ETON entered, +$19.32**; VERA was gap-below-floor anyway). **So it is NOT inert on the ranking side — but N=1 tradeable case is not a verdict either way.** ⚠ My first pass measured 12 scan-log HIGHs and got a clean null; the ranked population is the 141 queued rows, and on the right one the answer inverts — the same artifact as the 2026-07-19 "0 upgrades in 466 rows" read. **Re-measure after A lands, on post-move contested days.** §6 of the analysis doc. Refs #44, #415, #335, `scheduler.py:6650,7688`, `cross_strategy_allocator.py:97,241,252`. >> 🚢 **2026-10-10 — STEP A BUILT AND DEPLOYED (Sat, his 09-14 signature; Fable build, Opus review → ship, fixes applied):** the shadow allocator now ranks at **09:28 ET** (was 09:35, four minutes after the entries it ranked) against the **pre-entry LIVE book** — `db.get_open_position_count(account_mode='live', before_alert_date=D)`; the only caller is the allocator, the safeguard's own count (`live_tracker.count_open_positions`) is untouched, cap value unchanged (`safeguards.md` change log says so). Still shadow-only: places no orders; nothing live reads `shadow_rank` / `shadow_allocated`. Baseline: 11 of 20 non-empty mornings since 08-12 read ABOVE the pre-entry book (10-09: 5 vs 2 — two paper rehearsal rows + the 09:31 HUM fill counted, so HUM got 0 slots). EXPECT: (Mon 10-12) one `unified_allocation_decided` row with `ranked_at_et` 09:28:0x ET and a `ranked_ids` key (an empty queue writes `[]`); `mi_job_runs` started 09:28:00 ET, success. On the first non-empty morning: audit `open_positions` equals the rebuilt 09:28 live book (query in `docs/architecture/cross_strategy_allocator.md` 'Checking a morning live'). Unintended, by design: names first queued after 09:28 (the 09:31 upgrades) stay unranked — that is the signed Step A cost the Step B bar measures. DONE-WHEN: Step A is verified on the Mon 10-12 row (09:28 stamp + `ranked_ids`) and, on the first non-empty morning, `open_positions` equals the rebuilt book; then the line returns to the ladder build (its own DoD). WOULD-FAIL-IF: the row is stamped 09:35 or lacks `ranked_at_et` / `ranked_ids` (a stale execution image), or `open_positions` reads above the rebuilt book. VERIFY-LIVE Mon 10-12 ~09:30 ET (mi_audit_log + mi_job_runs). Step B only if A's incomplete-field cost shows in the data; the auto-demotion ladder (this line's DoD) is the later build.

This closes a blind spot the code NAMED — `compute_caller_cost_anomalies` is cold-start gated *"so a brand-new caller can't false-positive on day 2"*, and the reduction heuristics only ask whether EXISTING spend is reducible, never whether a lane should exist. 28 tests. ⚠ **DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT: a lane/purpose registry mapping each caller to what it buys** — it needs a "who consumes this" field nothing can verify, and #517 (filed TODAY) is live evidence that a registry without a forcing function becomes a pile. The weakest honest claim (*this started spending*) beats a richer one that rots. ✅ **VERIFIED-LIVE 2026-08-02 (deploy `0903575`, market-agent + execution).** Ran `compute_cost_watchdog` against REAL `api_usage` inside `apollo-market` (read-only — only the 17:52 ET job writes): clean run, 0 anomalies / 3 opportunities / **0 new lanes**, and CROSS-CHECKED against the raw table independently of the detector — the 5 most-recently-first-seen callers are all 6-17 days old, so zero is the CORRECT answer, not a silent failure. `judge_divergence` (first seen 7/27) is the worked example: it would have been announced on day one had this existed. ▶ **DEFERRED TO A WEEKDAY (flagged by the 8/02 /simplify altitude pass, NOT applied — money path, Sunday night, live judge grades at 09:45 ET):** the fix landed one layer too HIGH. `grade_holistic` now requires `log_caller`, but the true chokepoint is `judge_transport.invoke_forced_tool` — the function that actually calls `spend_tracker.log_anthropic_call_safe` — which still has `log_caller: Optional[str] = None`. **The residual hazard is WORSE than the one fixed**: a future forced-tool judge built directly on the transport (not via `grade_holistic`) would log NOTHING — no bucket at all, rather than the wrong bucket — and the AST test can't see it, because it hardcodes the three judge entry-point names. Verified: BOTH production call sites (`ep_grade_judge`, `mgmt_judge`) already pass a literal, so requiring it is zero-behaviour-change. ⚠ It is NOT free: `None = no logging` is a documented signed contract (#377, 'byte-identical to the pre-#377 path') with a test pinning it (`test_judge_logs_nothing_when_log_caller_none`), so this is a CONTRACT change, not a cleanup — it needs the deliberate deploy window, not a late-Sunday push. ⏭ ALSO CONSIDERED AND SKIPPED: moving the dedupe query into `db.py` per the CLAUDE.md single-SoT-for-queries rule. Two reviewers independently said leave it — `cost_board.py` already holds 3 inline cost-specific queries, and moving only the newest would split cost queries across two files, which is worse than the inconsistency it fixes. ▶ REMAINDER of #313 unchanged: replay-everything CI (#302) + spend-vs-P&L attribution.
